## Correction — EasyEDA SPICE pin field is not the physical pin number

Official EasyEDA Pin Attributes distinguishes Number (physical footprint pin number) from Spice Number (simulation-model pin mapping): https://docs.easyeda.com/en/SchematicLib/SchLib-Attributes/ . Earlier reviews treated c_spicepin differences as proof of duplicate/wrong physical pins. That conclusion was overstated and is withdrawn. Do not ask the user to renumber J_TEST5/GPS/ACS/LID/ALARM physicalpins just to match c_spicepin. Retain printed physical pin order and actual header/module fit; nativephysicalpadmappingisnotprovenorrejectedbysimulationindexdifferencesalone. Organized review.json now records this correction without deleting original observations. This doesnotwaive true power-net/measurement-feed issues or establishphysicalPASS.

Exact organizedSVGlabelcorrections: HCWOUT+ +5V_TO_ESP32→+5V_HCW; HCWOUT−GND_TO_ESP32→GND; PZEM+VB LARGE_SCREW_+LOAD→PZEM_FUSED_POSITIVE (actualsourceafterappropriatefuse); PZEMDC− LARGE_SCREW_-LOAD→SHUNT_SOURCE_NEGATIVE (actualSOURCEside). Keep selectedboard+5Vseparatefromraw+5V_HCW and explicitlyshowretainedreviewedremovablefeedbetweenthem, not a permanent USB/external5Vjoin. Renaming alone doesnotshowthatselection/feed.

Keep organizedalarmprinted1GPIO27/2GND and matchingoffboardcallouts; do not rewire to oldguide1GND/2signalwithoutchangingbothends. CurrentPZEMcommunication+5V/GNDnamedconnectionsarecorrect; currentSVG1+5V/4GND numberingdiffersfromoldsymbol,physicalterminalnamesgovern. No GPIOchange or blanketpinrework needed. No website/firmware/hardwarechangeinthisclarification.

## 25 October — simplified DP1 daily energy tables (local revision, publication unavailable)

User rejects the earlier ideal-nameplate-hour comparison and asks for slide-ready daily generation, consumption, charging contribution, range and riding-time computations for both prototype and BEMAC. The first Calculations card now contains concise solar-only and future stationary-hybrid tables, with simple substitutions and a primary-source basis below closed disclosures. The detailed measurement derivation and 36-input historical advanced audit remain in a separate appendix; component/protection justification remains its own card. Existing 39 numbered cards, original artwork, schematics and app/firmware are preserved.

Chosen theoretical case: actual reported 400 W array, 4 PSH, 70% array-to-battery-bus yield, 10 W total average battery-side electronics budget for 24h. 70% is an engineering allowance illustrated by NREL V5 14% non-thermal loss, standard-module −0.47%/°C, an ASSUMED 45°C cell temperature and ASSUMED 90% controller efficiency: 0.86×0.906×0.90=0.701244. The NREL method informs the scenario; it does not certify actual MPT efficiency. JICA/DOE 2004 printed p.143 gives historical annual Philippine insolation context 4.5–5.5kWh/m²/day; selected4PSH is not dated Caloocan plane-of-array weather. 10W is explicitly a chosen load budget, not measured consumption, a fuse-sizing basis or an enforced maximum.

Prototype retains a separately CHOSEN four-clock-hour effective window at a PROPOSED2A output cap and nominal48V: min(280W,96W)×4=384Wh source, minus240Wh electronics gives144Wh/day /30% nominal-energy equivalent. PSH alone cannot determine clipped charging duration. BEMAC full-array-compatible-interface reference:1120−240=880Wh/day /19.93% nominal-energy equivalent. No actual saved cap, current, SOC, chemical storage or charging-interface acceptance is inferred. The prototype pack is not a traction supply; full batteries curtail energy.

## 23 October — available capacitors clarified; proposed filter not installable as written

User reports a 104 ceramic, 470 µF and 1000 µF electrolytics rated 25 V, and a 100 µF / 25 V electrolytic. Quantity of 104 ceramics is not established. Standard 104 code means 100,000 pF = 100 nF = 0.1 µF; it establishes capacitance, not voltage rating. Thus the specified bypass capacitance is present, while the proposed 10 µF ceramic is not. Do not silently substitute the large electrolytics at A1: uncharacterized leakage through the existing approximately 5 kohm source resistance could create a significant offset, and time constant changes. The proposed 3.2 Hz filter remains unbuilt/unverified; no repeat capture or calibration enable is requested.

Clarify switch language: 24 V DC is the minimum proposed switch voltage rating, not a request to power the circuit at 24 V. The switch is only intended to interrupt bank positive while ACS stays powered. Existing records identify the user's CNLONQ/LONQ 4B rotary DC isolator as a possible existing switch; last guidance includes model-specific bridges and a cold terminal-path check, not a completed physical verification. Do not assume its presence means correctly wired/available or improvise terminal pairs from a generic toggle. No new purchase or LONQ rewiring is requested in this clarification. Inventory access used existing M6_ISOLATOR_CLARIFICATION_2026-10-21.md and SHARED_BATTERY_CARD_2026-10-22.md. No physical change/result/PASS.

Primary capacitance-code example: https://datasheets.kyocera-avx.com/AutoCapArray.pdf . Electrolytic leakage behavior: https://www.chemi-con.co.jp/en/faq/detail.php?id=alElectricalCh . These explain the component distinction; they do not identify the user's exact capacitor voltage rating, series or leakage.

Implemented bounded same-port reopen: after >=4096bytes and >=25% non-text bytes with no valid startup report for2s (or10s after a prior valid report), reopen the same selected port/settings **once per connection**, release DTR/RTS false, reset decoder/partial-line state and await a complete strict frame. Do not change baud/device, deliberately assert reset, accept corrupted bytes, close the app session or loop reopens. Commands are blocked during the internal reopen. Adapter-induced reset behavior and the physical cause of the byte corruption remain unproved. Browser/node fixtures are not physical adapter acceptance.

PPP may dial with an unset modem RTC, then must obtain usable ESP32 UTC before TLS. Legacy modemHTTPS still requires its clock. HTTPS Date is strictly parsed only after the certificate-verified response and corrects >2s skew; malformed dates are ignored. TLS certificate/time checks stay enabled. LTE Start/Once now fetch backend `/api/health.server_time` and send that UTC, not browser wall time; missing server time sends no command. Current user report alone does not prove exactly which clock supplied every rejected timestamp.

409 was previously always labelled payload_identity_conflict. Server now keeps the original strict409 behavior and emits/audits distinct `stale_sequence` vs `payload_identity_conflict`;400future rejections include `future_timestamp`. Firmware decodes only bounded, whitelisted rejection codes, including chunked bodies, without logging raw replies. It does not acknowledge rejected frames or weaken replay checks. Older LTE frames arriving after a newer USB record can be rejected as stale; the user's particular409 was not previously audited, so do not claim its exact cause established. ## Implemented software

Existing Next16.4/React19 premium frontend; Express backend with SQLite development and PostgreSQL/Supabase support; driver/operator/engineer/admin roles, authenticated USB/Wi-Fi ingress, strict versioned telemetry, atomic persistence, replay/conflict handling, CPE Lab and quality-aware module fields. Hosted deployment is not established by local tests.

Canonical firmware is firmware/solair-cpe; final entry solair_cpe.ino. Arduino IDE versions live in firmware/arduino-ide, generated by scripts/package-cpe-arduino-ide.py. There are currently14 IDE folders including an additional independent ADS comparison. Private solair_config.h files are retained locally, ignored by Git and not copied by packaging. Each test/final sketch has its own configuration. Never paste a whole sketch into modules.h.

All requested modules have software implementations: IMU, lid/mount switches, GPIO27 alarm/LED19, ADS1115, ACS758, NEO-6M, A7670E AT/packet interfaces and PZEM-017/50A shunt/RS485. Unverified electrical/transport interfaces remain disabled by default; implemented is not commissioned.

Pin map: I2C SDA21/SCL22; lid25/mount26 INPUT_PULLUP; alarm27 active-HIGH; status LED19 via330ohm resistor; ACS ADC1 GPIO34 after conditioning; GPS software RX32; RS485 UART1 RX16/TX17/DIR23; LTE UART2 RX13/TX14 only after level/pinout verification. Do not change this map casually.

## Reproducible cloud setup

Use Node24 and current repository paths; do not assume the Windows .tools environment exists. From repository root in a Linux workspace:

```sh
cd next-platform/solair-platform
node --version
npm ci
npm run typecheck
npm test
```

Start the app from that directory using npm run dev. It uses port3000; use the cloud environment's preview/port mechanism rather than assuming a user's local localhost reaches the cloud. This is development startup, not a public deployment. New machines do not have the original working database/accounts. Do not replace the bench database with fixtures.

Firmware compilation, if requested: inspect Python availability, create an isolated environment and install PlatformIO6.1.18. Use firmware/solair-cpe/platformio.ini (espressif32@6.10.0, esp32dev/Arduino2.0.17) and network access needed for its pinned packages. Use a writable short core/cache path appropriate to the actual OS; the old Windows .cpe-tools junction is excluded. Run compile-only targets; a cloud compile cannot establish a physical test or access this PC's USB by assumption.

Historical software evidence: full app suite42/42 SQLite and42/42 PGlite before the newest compatible-profile test; that additional focused CPE suite5/5 passed on each adapter. Typecheck/focused lint passed after the profile UI change. Final/integrated/all-enabled firmware and affected IMU/ADS targets compiled;05a v2 compiled separately. See dated logs in docs/cpe-evidence. Re-run appropriate checks in the new environment; do not claim they already ran there. Browser/production-build results are earlier checkpoints, not tests of an uploaded cloud environment.

## What must remain local/private

Git excludes private env/firmware configs, working data and databases, node_modules, .next/.pio/.tools, the root .cpe-tools junction, account/session files and key files. Ignored config contains real device/Wi-Fi provisioning when supplied; recreate privately on each bench computer. Hardware uploads, siren activation and physical measurements require the actual connected equipment. Back up research records separately through an explicit private database-backup workflow; Git is not that backup.
